# IvritSuite Improvement Log

The memory of the continuous-improvement loop. **Read this first every session.** One concern per
iteration, one commit per iteration. Prioritization: P1 (data loss/security/broken core/export
corruption) > P2 (silently wrong output/undo holes/a11y blockers) > P3 (perf/dead UI/confusing
copy/consistency) > P4 (polish). Tie-breakers: (1) affects teachers' saved work, (2) affects the
printed/exported artifact a student receives, (3) dual-audience (Hebrew + secular) wins, (4) smallest diff.

## Candidates (prioritized, top = next)

- [ ] P4 (**NEW S295 Pass I — a REFINEMENT of S294's control rule, which as written would have thrown away a good arm**) | loop protocol | **A silent control has two causes, and only one of them condemns the arm.** S294 established "run a firing control first and abandon any arm whose control is silent". S295's raw-key control fired 0 — but the detector was fine; the **control string** was wrong (`control.raw.key` starts with a namespace outside the detector's prefix list, so no detector built on that list could ever match it). A real key shape fired 2/2 and the 26-load zero stood. **Rule as it should read: a silent control halts the arm until the SILENCE itself is diagnosed — bad control vs blind detector — and only a blind detector invalidates the zero.** The same run also threw away a whole dictionary probe for the opposite reason (a genuinely wrong selector, `.dict-empty` where the markup is `#resultsGrid .empty`), so both halves of the distinction were exercised in one session. | found: 2026-08-30 S295 Pass I

- [ ] P4 (**NEW S295 — two probe artifacts that both LOOK like defects; recorded so no future run re-files them**) | loop protocol | **(1) `textContent` does not respect `aria-hidden`.** Reading a tile's `textContent` to check its accessible name reported `שָׁלוֹם1` — the `aria-hidden` number badge included — which reads as an a11y defect and is not one. Strip `[aria-hidden="true"]` from a clone (or use the AX tree) before judging an accessible name. **(2) A raw-i18n-key scan over `innerHTML` counts `data-i18n` ATTRIBUTES.** A hint row with 4 `data-i18n` attributes reports `rawKeys: 4` while its rendered text is perfectly localized. Scan rendered text nodes and user-visible attributes (`title`/`aria-label`/`placeholder`), never raw `innerHTML`. | found: 2026-08-30 S295 micro-feature verification

- [ ] P4 (**NEW S294 Pass D — a MEASURED decision NOT to act, recorded so no future D re-derives it**) | Hebrew_Font_Maker.html | **Generalizing the S294 hidden-tab guard to the other nine cat-panel renderers is NOT worth it, measured.** With every optional category toggled ON (custom glyphs, special/wide forms, Yiddish, Ladino) and `catTab='letter'`, the remaining hidden-panel rebuild per letter click at 4× CPU is **~19ms total** — `renderPunctPanel` 8.3ms, `renderLadinoPanel` 7.6ms, every other panel under 2ms — against the 130ms `renderSpacingPanel` alone cost before the fix. The spacing panel was the outlier *because* its kerning list is unbounded; the rest are bounded by fixed glyph inventories. Nine more guards, each needing its own show-path, to buy ~19ms at 4× (~5ms at 1×) is a bad trade. | found: 2026-08-30, S294 Pass D panel census
- [ ] P4 (**NEW S294 Pass D — an architectural cost, logged not fixed; the debounce is what makes it acceptable**) | Hebrew_Font_Maker.html | **`autosaveNow()` re-serializes the WHOLE project, images included, on every autosave.** `JSON.stringify(project)` is synchronous and main-thread; measured at 4× CPU: a 2.43MB project (one ~1.5MB page-scan dataUrl + 27 × 30KB crops + contours) → one **233ms** block; a 5.77MB project → **406ms** for the stringify alone, `autosaveNow` **1103ms** end-to-end. **Why this is NOT a defect:** `markDirty()` clears and re-arms its 3s timer on every edit, so the autosave never fires *during* continuous work — only ~3s after the user pauses, where a hitch is invisible. `stripImagesForAutosave` (which exists precisely because images dominate) is used ONLY on the localStorage fallback path, never on the primary IndexedDB one. A real fix is incremental/structured storage — well past a loop iteration. | found: 2026-08-30, S294 Pass D autosave arm
- [ ] P4 (**NEW S294 Pass D — a defensive-only note, explicitly NOT a live bug; grounded before filing**) | Hebrew_Font_Maker.html | **`stripImagesForAutosave` reads `l.source.dataUrl` with no guard on `l.source`**, inside the `catch` that is the last line of defence when IndexedDB is unavailable (private mode) — and its own `try` swallows the throw, so a failure there loses the autosave silently. **It cannot fire today:** `blankLetter` always sets `source`, and `migrateProject` re-asserts `ex.source = ex.source || {…}` on every load, so every reachable project has it. Filed only so a future refactor that relaxes either guarantee knows this reader is unguarded on a data-loss path. | found: 2026-08-30, S294 Pass D


- [ ] P4 (**NEW S293 Pass G — recorded so the next FM export audit starts here instead of re-deriving the bridge**) | Hebrew_Font_Maker.html | **The UFO export path (`hfm_build_ufo`/`buildUfoFontInfo`) sets no embedding bits at all** — `openTypeOS2Type` appears nowhere in the page, so a UFO handed to an external compiler inherits *that* tool's default rather than the `fsType=0` the TTF path now writes (`7b25835`). **[S293 post-close-out UPDATE — MEASURED, no longer hypothetical]** the adversarial review of S293's diff pinned the actual value: **ufo2ft 3.9.0's `getAttrWithFallback` returns `[2]` for an absent `openTypeOS2Type`**, i.e. the standard fontmake/ufo2ft toolchain compiles the exported UFO to **`fsType=4`** — the very restriction `7b25835` removed from the TTF path, and on a CC0 or partner-OFL project it contradicts the LICENSE.txt shipped beside it. So the divergence is now a measured TTF/UFO inconsistency **created by this session's own fix**, not a vague inheritance. Still not shipped: a UFO is an intermediate a font engineer re-compiles deliberately, and the one-line fix (`openTypeOS2Type: []` in the fontinfo dict) was offered to the maintainer and not authorized. **Take it the next time the Font Maker is in scope.** Not a defect in the shipped TTF and **not** worth a speculative fix — a UFO is an intermediate a font engineer re-compiles deliberately — but if the UFO path is ever audited, this is the known divergence. **Also recorded: the S293 bridge is reproducible and cheap.** jsdelivr is still 403 in this container (re-receipted S293) and `python3` ships no fontTools, but **PyPI is reachable**: `pip install pymupdf fonttools uharfbuzz` gives fontTools **4.63.0**, and `PY_BUILDER` is pure Python (zero Pyodide coupling) while `buildFontSpec()` is pure JS — so any future session can compile and inspect the tool's real artifact in minutes. Extract `PY_BUILDER` with `page.evaluate(() => PY_BUILDER)`, never by regexing the template literal out of the HTML.

- [ ] P3 (**NEW S292 Pass H — the pass's headline finding; GATE 2 ASKED, maintainer chose "log it only, change nothing"**) | hebrew_dictionary.html | **"⭑ Save as Word List…" is discoverable only from a theme.** Word lists are the hub of the suite's whole cross-tool pipeline — `?wl=` feeds both the generator worksheet and the flash-cards drill — and there are three ways in, of which only two are findable. The **theme** path (pick a theme → `themeSaveWlBtn` appears in the Themes panel) and the **toolbar** manager (`#dictWordListsBtn` "📚 Word Lists") are both in plain sight. But a teacher who assembles a set the way the tool actually invites — **by search, by filter, or by root**, which is the point of having 13,081 searchable words — must reach `#wlSaveBtn`, which sits **two collapses deep**: inside sub-section `#copyOptsSec`, inside the `#panelAdvanced` panel, both collapsed by default. Measured chain: `#wlSaveBtn` → `div` → `.adv-section-body` (`display:none`) → `#copyOptsSec.adv-section.collapsed` → `.panel-body` (`display:none`) → `#panelAdvanced.panel.collapsed`. **The label is the sharper half of the problem:** that section is titled **"Copy Options"** but holds *three* capabilities — copy modes, Anki/Quizlet export, and Word Lists — so the one control that matters most to the pipeline is filed under a heading that names only the least important of the three. Compounding it, `.adv-section` collapse is **NOT** covered by panel-collapse memory (CLAUDE.md: only `.panel`), so the teacher re-expands it every visit. **Two fix shapes were put to the maintainer and both declined for now:** rename the heading to cover its contents (1 CSV key pair, smallest real change), or add a second save entry point outside Advanced. | found: 2026-08-30, S292 Pass H
- [ ] P3 (**NEW S292 Pass H — a mechanism, measured to the codepoint; the fix shape is gate-2 or the seed below, so it is logged not shipped**) | hebrew_dictionary.html | **A pointed Hebrew search that is one mark off dead-ends with no recovery, and the page's own keyboard invites exactly that input.** The corpus is normalized on both sides — `buildWord` stores `word` (pointed) **and** `bare = word.replace(/[֑-ׇ]/g,'')` — but **the query is never normalized**: the filter is `!w.word.includes(q) && !w.bare.includes(q) && …`, so a pointed query is compared **byte-for-byte** against `w.word`. Measured: `כתב` → **24 words** (matches `bare`); the corpus's own exact `כָּתַב` → **2**; the same word missing only the kaf's dagesh, `כָתַב` (`5db 5b8 5ea 5b7 5d1`) → **"No words found"**. This is not a contrived path: the on-screen keyboard shipped on this very search box carries a **Nikkud tab** whose whole purpose is letting a keyboard-less teacher type pointed Hebrew, and pasting a pointed word out of a chumash or siddur is the commonest "what does this mean?" journey there is. The empty state is otherwise good — magnifier, message, and two recovery actions — but **both actions ("Clear search", "Reset all filters") discard the teacher's typing, and neither is the recovery that applies here**. **Why it was not simply fixed:** the naive one-line fix (also test `bare` against a nikkud-stripped query) is a *semantics* change, not a bug fix — it would make `כָּתַב` return 24 instead of 2, destroying the precision a teacher who deliberately typed full pointing was buying. The non-destructive shape is the Feature seed logged below. | found: 2026-08-30, S292 Pass H **[S293 UPDATE — MITIGATED, NOT CLOSED]** `4feafa4` shipped the empty-state recovery ("Search without vowels", the seed this candidate was routed to), so a teacher who hits the dead end now has a one-click way out. **The mechanism itself is unchanged and deliberately so** — matching a stripped query against `bare` would make a fully-pointed search return 24 instead of 2. Keep this open as the record of WHY the obvious fix is wrong; do not re-file it as new.

- [ ] P4 (**NEW S291 Pass M — a REFUTATION, and a caution about grep-shaped findings**) | suite-wide (14 root HTML files) | **The CSS `color-scheme` property is declared NOWHERE in the suite — and that turns out not to matter.** Two traps here, both worth keeping. Second, the corrected premise (no `color-scheme`, so Chromium treats the page as light-scheme) does NOT produce the expected consequence: pixel-diffing four controls (submit button, pill, footer link, text input) focused vs unfocused in dark mode shows **Chromium paints its adaptive two-tone focus ring and it is plainly legible** — a white ring on the navy submit button. - [x] ✅ DONE S287 iter 2, `5197b1e` (fixed TOGETHER with the P2 below — one clamp table at `applySettings`, exactly as both candidates predicted). **S287 CORRECTS THE REACH CLAIMED HERE:** an app-GENERATED share code cannot carry a non-finite value — `shareCodeEncode` is `JSON.stringify`, which emits `null` for Infinity — so the vector is a **hand-crafted** payload (`{"mode":1e999}` is valid JSON), not one the app produces. Severity stands (a pasted code from an untrusted source freezes the tab unrecoverably); the *provenance* was overstated. | flash_cards.html | **A share code / `?s=` link carrying a non-finite `mode` HANGS the tab permanently.** `applySettings({...getSettings(), mode: JSON.parse('1e999')})` blocks the main thread indefinitely — probed from OUTSIDE the blocked thread (schedule the call on a `setTimeout`, then poll from Playwright): the page was unresponsive at **t = 3, 6, 10, 15, 22 and 30s and never recovered**, so this is an unbounded loop, not a slow render. **Reach is the worrying part: `shareCodeDecode` returns `ivritSafeParse(...)` — prototype-safe but with NO numeric clamping — so a pasted teacher share code or a clicked `?s=` link carries it, not just a hand-edited `.ivrit`.** The other 8 numeric keys were isolated one-per-context and all complete in 4–5ms, so `mode` is the sole hang vector. **Fix shape (mirroring `sanitizeFontNumbers`, S286 iter 4): clamp on the READ side — coerce `mode` to a valid enum member and every numeric to its setter's range at the `applySettings`/decode boundary, before anything loops on it.** Do this FIRST next session. | found: 2026-08-29, S286 Pass A registration sweep

Flash cards shares the Font Maker's exposure shape — these live in a **plain JS settings object**, not in a DOM control, so nothing coerces them; `timeLimitSeconds: Infinity` is a timer that never expires and `numberMax: Infinity` is an unbounded sampling range. **Fix alongside the P1 above — one clamp table at the same boundary closes both.** | found: 2026-08-29, S286 Pass A registration sweep

- [ ] P4 (**NEW S286 iter 5 — a STRUCTURAL result worth keeping, so no future sweep re-derives it**) | generator + dashboard (vs flash_cards + Font Maker) | **Why two tools are immune to the non-finite class and two are not: the DOM is the guard.** An imported numeric written into `<input type=number>` is REJECTED outright (value → `""`, `+value` → 0) and one written into `<input type=range>` is clamped to that input's own min/max — measured on 14 generator and 15 dashboard inputs, and confirmed end-to-end (0 non-finite survivors, 22ms / 36ms). The two exposed tools are exactly the two that hold their numerics in a **plain JS object read directly by a build/render path** (`project.font.*` → `buildFEA`; the flash-cards settings blob). **So the detector for this class is not "does it parse untrusted JSON" but "does the number reach a consumer without passing through a form control".** | found: 2026-08-29, S286

- [ ] P3 (**NEW S263 Pass M** -- a real offline/PWA defect, LOGGED NOT FIXED because this container could not fetch the assets) | torah_trainer.html + index.html | **The Sefaria attribution logo is hotlinked from `files.readme.io`.** Two `<img>` (light + dark variants) point at `https://files.readme.io/87c5652-image.png` and `.../dcee0a8-image.png` -- a third-party DOCUMENTATION-asset host, not Sefaria's own domain -- and the origin is CSP-allowlisted in `img-src` on both pages, so it is deliberate, not an accident. Two consequences: (1) **cross-origin requests bypass the service worker (CLAUDE.md, Service Worker section), so the logo cannot work offline in an installed PWA** -- the footer renders a broken-image icon beside the alt text, which is exactly the "does this page look cared for" question pass M exists to ask; (2) a `files.readme.io/<hash>-image.png` URL is an ephemeral doc-host asset path that can rot at any time, taking the attribution mark with it. **The suite already has the opposite convention written down: the Starting Fonts intake vendors partner assets as "vendored same-origin copies, never hotlinked".** Fix = vendor both PNGs next to the other static media, repoint the two `<img>`, drop `files.readme.io` from both CSPs, and (optionally) precache them. **HONEST BLOCKER: this container's egress proxy returns HTTP 403 (CONNECT tunnel failed) for `files.readme.io`, so live reachability could not be verified and the bytes could not be downloaded here. RE-TESTED S287 (Pass E) — STILL BLOCKED: both URLs return `curl (56) CONNECT tunnel failed, response 403`, and `$HTTPS_PROXY/__agentproxy/status` names it a gateway **policy denial** for `files.readme.io:443`, not a transient failure. So this is an environment-policy blocker, not a flaky one — a future session should NOT burn an iteration re-testing it unless the network policy changes. Repointing the `<img>` without the bytes would render a BROKEN image, which is strictly worse than the current hotlink, so the half-fix was deliberately not shipped. A session with working egress -- or the maintainer dropping the two files in -- can finish this in one small commit.** Attribution wording is Sefaria's credit line, so if any visible text changes it is **gate 2**; swapping the image source alone is not.

- [ ] P4 (**NEW S263 iter 5** -- a METHOD finding that RETRACTED a P2 this session had already half-written against itself; the most reusable thing the run produced) | pass M / C / N protocol | **A pixel-sampling contrast probe measures the brightest ANTIALIASED pixel, and therefore under-reports a THIN glyph under `-webkit-font-smoothing: antialiased`.** The dashboard's fullscreen exit ✕ measured 2.98:1 light / 3.77:1 dark on a page whose CSS is provably correct (plate 0.72, no group opacity, `#fff`, opacity 1) -- the page that FIXED this very pattern at S248. Group opacity, ancestor `filter`/`mix-blend`/`isolation`, `backdrop-filter` and plate translucency were each ruled out by forcing them off; the brightest pixel in the glyph box stayed `rgb(110,113,121)` even over an opaque black plate. **Cause: the dashboard sets `-webkit-font-smoothing: antialiased` page-wide while torah and the generator resolve `auto`; everything else about the glyph is byte-equal (Arial, 24px, w400, `#fff`, same 20x27 ink box, same U+2715).** `antialiased` drops subpixel rendering and renders thin strokes lighter, so peak coverage is ~43%, not 100%. **RULE: a low contrast reading on a thin stroke is not a finding until a shape-aware or visual check agrees.** Corollary, and the reason this did not invalidate the sibling fix: iteration 3's generator numbers moved 1.78 -> 7.95 on the SAME probe, SAME page, SAME `auto` smoothing, with before/after crops showing a ghost becoming a solid plate -- a delta measured against itself is trustworthy where an absolute threshold across pages is not.

(4) **What actually works: `classList.toggle('<mark>', <expr> === <shared current value>)`** - the two-arg toggle whose condition is an equality test is the mutually-exclusive-selector signature, and it self-controls (27 sites in the suite DO write aria on that path, so a zero is provably a real zero). **Then the source result MUST be re-checked live**, because the aria is often written far from the toggle (see the sibling note below) - source flagged 86 sites, live cleared all but one. | found: 2026-08-26, S258 Pass A

- [ ] P4 (**NEW S253 Pass D** — a REFUTED optimization, recorded so no future D/perf session re-derives and ships it) | hebrew_dictionary.html | **Building each `loadMore` batch into a `DocumentFragment` is NOT a win — it is noise.** The obvious read of `loadMore()` is that it appends 100 cards one at a time into the live grid, 131 times, and that batching them through a fragment would cut the invalidation cost. A/B'd on the full 13,081-card expansion: 1× showed **−22%**, but the run-to-run variance on *identical* code was larger than the effect (current measured 1,673ms then 1,106ms), and 4× showed **−4%**. Nothing was changed. **The teardown was the real target on the same path** — and there `replaceChildren` is a 23–42× win, because ~13,000 *removals* are genuinely expensive where ~13,000 *appends* are not. | found: 2026-08-25, S253 Pass D

- [ ] P4 (**NEW S253 Pass D** — a METHOD finding about the loop's own probes, and the reason one finding was nearly killed) | pass D / G / M / N protocol | **Reading a CSS property at t=0 measures the transition's START, not the state.** S253's first print-media read of `#appToast` reported `opacity: 0` for a toast that demonstrably prints, because the read happened in the same task as the `showAppToast()` call — before the opacity transition had run. At t=300ms, and with a fresh toast under print media, it is `opacity: 1`. **Any probe that asserts on a transitioned property must wait out the transition first**, and a "0" on an element that is `display:block` and `position:fixed` deserves a second look. This is the same family as S251's "read it AFTER the action" and S252's `emulateMedia({media:null})`-ordering slip — three sessions running, the probe's *timing* has been the defect. | found: 2026-08-25, S253 Pass D

Measured over **all 787 root chips**: **mean 14ms, exactly 1 root over 50ms (חרש, 60ms), 0 over 200ms.** `shoreshMatchCost` returns `Infinity` cheaply for almost every word, so the inner loop only ever runs on the handful that embed the root. **Do not "optimize" this** — and the same caution applies to reading the cost of any matcher whose fast path is a rejection. | found: 2026-08-25, S253 Pass D

- [ ] P4 (**NEW S247 Pass M** — a probe DEFECT that produced three false findings before it was caught; recorded because every future M/C contrast probe inherits it) | pass M / pass C protocol | **A contrast probe must exclude disabled controls and composite element `opacity`.** S247's first run flagged `#regenBtn`/`#printBtn`/`#pdfBtn` at 1.84:1 — all three carry `disabled` + `aria-disabled="true"`, and **WCAG 1.4.3 explicitly exempts inactive user-interface components**. Exclude via `el.closest('[disabled],[aria-disabled="true"]')`. Separately, cumulative ancestor `opacity` must multiply into the foreground alpha or a dimmed control reads as full-strength. **And the inverse trap:** the generator's dark disabled treatment (`opacity:1` + explicit `#3d4f6a`) is deliberate and MORE legible than light's `opacity:0.35` — so the naive probe was not merely noisy, it was pointing at the better of the two implementations.
- [ ] P4 (**NEW S247 Pass M** — a METHOD finding; the arithmetic and the pixels disagreed and the pixels were right) | pass M protocol | **`getComputedStyle` cannot see a `filter`, and `brightness()` scales the TEXT as well as the background.** Predicting a filtered hover's contrast by brightening only the background gave 6.04:1 where the rendered pixels measure 5.86:1. Any fix delivered via `filter` (the suite's dominant gold-button hover idiom, 7 carriers) must be verified by **sampling rendered pixels** — decode the screenshot and take the background as the modal colour and the ink as the pixel furthest from it in luminance. Taking the *darkest* pixel as the ink silently reports 1.00:1 when the label is lighter than its field, which is exactly the failing case.

- [ ] P4 (**NEW S246 Pass M** — a METHOD finding, and the SECOND correction this detector has needed in two sessions) | pass A / pass M protocol | **The `row-siblings` detector needs vertical-OVERLAP clustering, not parent-element grouping.** S245 corrected S225's `round(top/6)` bucketing (which split one centre-aligned row into tidy buckets — a false NEGATIVE) by grouping strictly by the parent element. That over-corrected: on a **wrapping grid** the parent holds several visual rows, so index.html's 8-card `.cards` grid reported a 43px "spread" that is just row 1 being shorter than row 3 — a false POSITIVE, and the fix S245 prescribed is what produced it. Corrected definition, used for this session's census: group by parent, then cluster children whose vertical extents **overlap**; centre-aligned siblings always overlap (one row), grid rows never do (separate rows), and no rounding appears anywhere. Verified both ways on index.html: the `.cards` false positive disappears and the real `.hright` finding survives.

The registered pattern is about **peer controls** in a control row; matching a star to a heading is meaningless. Either restrict the detector to control-like children or expect to ground one false positive per page — this one was killed by reading the markup, not by inspection of the numbers.

A transparent `::after` carrying the pointer target instead reaches 24px with the paint untouched. **Two traps, both hit this session:** (1) the `::after` must NOT inherit the badge's `border-radius: 50%` — that makes the target a 24px CIRCLE whose corners fail 0/4; it is invisible, so a square is strictly better. (2) For an element pinned to a viewport edge, expansion must be INWARD ONLY and use LOGICAL insets — `.sidebar-show-btn` sits at x=0 in English and x=1264 in Hebrew, so a symmetric expansion would push half the target off-screen in both. Verify by hit-testing at +4px and +7px past the paint WITH a control at +12px that must fail. | found: 2026-08-22, S240

- [x] ✅ DONE 2026-08-21, `PENDING` (maintainer said "do the punctuation fix too" — shipped in the same v5.18 release) | Hebrew_Font_Maker.html | **Untraced punctuation and digits export as blank cmapped glyphs instead of falling back.** `buildFontSpec` pass 1 emits every `LETTERS` entry unconditionally, so a font whose digits were never traced still ships `uni0030`–`uni0039` with empty contours — typing `5` renders nothing rather than substituting a fallback face. Fixing it means skipping contour-less `cat:'punct'` entries in pass 1 **and** in `emitBaseAnchors`' `LETTERS` loop in lockstep: that loop calls `seedAnchorsIfNeeded`, so a glyph dropped from `glyphOrder` would still get `pos base` lines and **feaLib would reject the whole FEA**. Once that holds, the 21 ASCII symbols the app models nowhere (`# $ % & * + / < = > @ [ \ ] ^ _ ` { | } ~` — present in both starting fonts, and the reason an exported font still can't type a full English sentence) can be added as `cat:'punct'` rows and ride the existing import loop with no new import code. Adding them WITHOUT the pass-1 fix would ship 21 more blank glyphs and make `@` render as nothing — which is why v5.18 shipped letters only.

At 1× the same ladder is 0 / 51 / 86 / **139 ms** — i.e. it crosses the bar only under throttle, the same class as S209's 446 ms boot chunk. **The cause was split rather than assumed:** an in-page microbench at 4× puts the whole-pool-per-card `rwShuffle` at **48 ms** of the 834 (a partial Fisher-Yates over only `cellsNeeded` positions would be 8 ms) and the *detached* build of 200×25 cells at **60 ms** — so ~85 % of the block is style+layout of 5,000 live CSS-grid cells, which the worksheet's chunked-build machinery would defer but not remove. Not fixed: the tractable 48 ms is 6 % of the block, and chunking the remainder is a rewrite of `generateRealWordsBingo`'s page loop, well past one-concern scope. Mitigation already shipped: `liveIsHeavyMode()` flags `rwBingoCards > 40`, so live preview never re-triggers it. | found: 2026-08-16, S220 Pass D

- [x] ✅ DONE S221, 40b56a3 (**took the SECOND of the two fix shapes this entry offered — drop the `size` keyword, keep `landscape` — rather than importing the generator's Letter/A4 setting into a tool that has no page-size control at all; flash-cards was no longer variety-blocked, exactly as the S220 pointer noted**) | P4 (**NEW S219 Pass G** — a real divergence from the suite's own precedent, logged rather than fixed because flash-cards hit the 2-per-tool cap that session) | flash_cards.html | **The completion certificate hardcodes `@page { size: letter landscape }`, so it ignores paper size entirely.** `_certEnter` injects that rule and `_certExit` removes it (the injection/removal pair is correct and must stay — an `@page` rule cannot be scoped by a body class). The issue is the literal `letter`: the generator solved this exact problem with a `pageSize` setting and `_applyPageSizeStyle()`, which re-emits `@page { size: letter|A4 <orient> }`, and its comment notes it "matters in A4-locale browsers". A certificate forced to Letter on A4 paper is scaled to fit by the print dialog, so the failure is a silently smaller certificate rather than a lost one — hence P4, not P2. **Measured S219:** the injected rule wins over the requested paper in every cell (`page.pdf({format:'A4'})` still produced a Letter-landscape sheet, byte-identical to the Letter request). The card sheet and the progress report correctly declare no `@page` at all and paginate fine on both papers. Fix shape if taken: reuse the generator's Letter/A4 pattern, or drop the `size` keyword and keep only `landscape`. | found: 2026-08-15, S219 Pass G

- [x] ✅ RESOLVED S278 — REFUTED as a live defect with a 4-arm probe; **the prescribed fix ALREADY EXISTED as `a6bf328` (2026-08-02), 13 days BEFORE the S217 observation** (was: UNRESOLVED LEAD, NEW S217 Pass H) | torah_trainer.html | **"Chant all" with PocketTorah unreachable appears to leave the button reading "⏹ Stop chant" indefinitely with no message.** Observed: the button flips to its stop label, `#ttAudio.src` is set, and the label never reverts — but **`audio.error` stays `null` even when the MP3 is answered 403**, so the page's own failure hook may simply never fire. **This is logged as a lead, not a finding, because the measurement could not separate three candidate causes**: headless autoplay policy, Playwright route interception changing how the media element reports failure, and real product behaviour. Citing the **S165 precedent** — a fabricated "Flash Cards is silent on failure" finding that came from exactly this shape of under-grounded probe — the pass stopped short of calling it a defect. **Method notes for whoever takes it:** open the context with `serviceWorkers:'block'` (the worker otherwise serves `/data/` past the route), and prefer a *real* unreachable origin or a DNS-level block over route abort, since abort is the variable under suspicion; confirm on a real device before writing any code. If it does reproduce, the fix shape is the `_verseEndStopAt` watcher gaining an error/stall path that reverts the label and writes `setStatus`, matching the register the S214 convergence established. | found: 2026-08-15, S217 Pass H audio arm | **S278 resolution (chant_probe.mjs receipts):** under an autoplay-allowed launch (`--autoplay-policy=no-user-gesture-required`) + `serviceWorkers:'block'` + canned Sefaria v3, ALL THREE legitimate failure shapes — 404-fulfil, garbage-200, and route-abort (the S217 shape itself) — fire `currentAudio.onerror`: `audio.error.code=4`, button reverts to "🎵 Chant all", `chantAllState` clears, the localized status + toast show. The ok-audio CONTROL played through and reverted via `onended` — which also rules out autoplay-blocking in the probe, since a swallowed `play()` rejection reverts nothing. S217's `audio.error === null` observation was probe-shaped: its launch lacked the autoplay flag, so `play()` was blocked before any media fetch — no fetch, no error event, exactly the null it measured. No code change needed.

- [x] ✅ DONE S219, c2399f1 (**grounded exactly as this entry demanded, and the grounding SPLIT the two carriers — one fixed, one closed as not-applicable**) | P4 (**NEW S213 Pass C** — a plausible sibling of the shipped f207a34, deliberately logged rather than assumed) | Hebrew_Font_Maker.html | **Two U+25CC DOTTED CIRCLE carriers may be the same decorative-duplicate shape the Trope Tutor just fixed** — `qaCh` stack heads (~12817) and the glyph-tile builder (~14790, whose own comment says "dotted circle ◌ carries an isolated" mark). Needs grounding before any fix: check whether each carrier sits beside its own mark NAME (decorative → `aria-hidden`) or IS the content (leave exposed, as trope's drill question is). FM has 5 occurrences total; the other 3 are non-visual. | found: 2026-08-15, S213 Pass C (suite grep for the carrier) **S219 RESULT — the decision rule in this entry resolved both carriers: (a) the `qaColumns`/`qaRenderGrid` header ALREADY carried `aria-label`, so the `<th>` name was correct — but `aria-label` names the ELEMENT, not its subtree, and the carrier span plus its text node stayed in the tree: **34 dotted-circle names across 51 nodes** in the header's accessibility tree, measured with `interestingOnly:false`. Marked the span `aria-hidden` per the f207a34 call-site rule → **0 across 17**, one name per column, glyph still visibly rendered. (b) `templateCellHTML` (~14797) is **NOT an accessibility surface at all** — it feeds `renderTemplateCanvases`, so its dotted circle is ink on a printed practice sheet. Closed as not-applicable, not fixed. **Method note that nearly cost the verdict:** the first snapshot returned `[]` names and the check PASSED on an empty set — `page.accessibility.snapshot()` defaults to `interestingOnly:true` and prunes the very nodes this pattern lives in. Always assert the snapshot is non-empty before trusting a zero.

- [x] ✅ DONE (**closed by the S222–S230 reflow campaign; the 26/26-arm suite receipt has existed since S230 and this candidate outlived it — flipped at the S232 close-out with a FRESH 26-arm re-measure over the S231 footer convergence + S232 changes: 13 pages × EN/HE at 320px, docOverflow 0 and 0 pageerrors on every arm**) | P3 (**NEW S213 Pass C** — measured suite-wide, cause NOT yet attributed, so no fix is proposed) | suite-wide (4 of 5 pages measured) | **Two-dimensional scrolling at 320 CSS px — a WCAG 1.4.10 (Reflow, AA) miss.** Measured `scrollWidth` vs `clientWidth` at a 320px viewport: trope_tutor 394/320, torah_trainer 477/320, hebrew_dictionary 378/320, flash_cards 336/320; **index.html is clean at 320/320**. The dictionary's overflow attributes cleanly to `div.hright` (the header button row) at exactly its scrollWidth; the other three do NOT — their widest boxes sit inside the off-canvas settings drawer and exceed the measured scrollWidth, so the detector's "worst offender" list is NOT the cause there. **Whoever takes this must attribute the overflow before changing anything** (bisect by hiding candidates, or use `document.elementsFromPoint` at the right edge). Cross-tool, so it is pass-F/G shaped, not a one-tool C fix. | found: 2026-08-15, S213 Pass C reflow arm — **UPDATED S217: the demand was met for torah_trainer, and it has TWO causes, one now closed.** Attribution was done exactly as this entry required (bisect by hiding candidates → deepest-culprit walk → right-edge `elementsFromPoint`), and it named two independent contributors, not one: **(1) `select#parshaSelect` inside a `min-width:auto` flex item — torah-specific, FIXED S217 `30bd836`** (`.tt-ctrl{min-width:0}` + `.tt-ctrl select{min-width:110px;max-width:100%}`, 29/29 across 320/390/800/1280 × light/dark × empty/loaded); **(2) `div.hright` — the SAME element this entry already names for the dictionary, so it is the cross-tool half.** `.hright` is pinned `flex-shrink:0` with no `flex-wrap`, and the **11 `.hright` carriers have divergent rules**, so converging them is genuine cross-tool work and is what remains of this candidate. Note for whoever takes it: fixing one cause does not clear the page — torah still overflows at 320 on `.hright` alone, so re-measure per cause rather than treating a single fix as the close. — **SHARPENED S218 by an attempted-and-reverted fix (full entry in Done): `flex-shrink:0` is the blocker, and `flex-wrap` alone is INERT against it.** Adding `flex-wrap:wrap` to `.hright` changes nothing, because a `flex-shrink:0` container never shrinks below its max-content width (380px in torah), so its children never run out of room and never wrap. Any real fix must first establish **what `flex-shrink:0` is protecting** on each of the 11 carriers — that flag, not the missing wrap, is the actual cross-tool question. Second trap, measured: wrapping the **header** instead is not a safe substitute — unscoped it regresses 800px, where the subtitle (hidden only ≤720px) pushes the header over the width and wrap engages, growing it 77→112px; today `.htitle{flex:1;min-width:0}` absorbs that by truncating. So a correct torah fix is at minimum *two* concerns (breakpoint-scoped header wrap + the `flex-shrink` question) and should not be attempted as a single iteration. — **LARGELY RESOLVED S223 (`0201c95`); read this before touching the two pages that remain.** Two things changed. **(1) The attribution above was WRONG and is superseded.** Re-measured at 320px with off-canvas content *properly excluded* (walk each ancestor; treat a `position:fixed` element with a non-zero `translateX`, or any `display:none`/`visibility:hidden` ancestor, as not page content), the header button row is the sole on-canvas cause on **five of six** pages, its right edge equal to `scrollWidth` to the pixel: trope 394, torah 451, dashboard 408, dictionary 378, flash_cards `.h-right` 336. The old "the other three do NOT attribute to `.hright`" reading was the **closed settings drawer being measured as page content** — the exclusion filter is the whole difference, so any future overflow census must carry one. **The generator is the one genuine exception**: its 344 is `aside#appSidebar.sidebar` (width 344), never named before. **(2) The lever S218 was missing: `max-width:100%`.** S218 is right that `flex-wrap` is inert against `flex-shrink:0` — but a flex row wraps once something **caps** its width, and `max-width:100%` does exactly that **without touching `flex-shrink`**, so the buttons still cannot squash. `flex-wrap:wrap;max-width:100%;justify-content:flex-end` (all direction-relative, RTL-safe) cleared trope 74→0, dictionary 58→0 and flash_cards 16→0 with **zero** change at ≥768px. **Scope it per file to that file's own narrow breakpoint** — unscoped it is inert on trope and flash_cards but shifts the dictionary header at 390–1024px. **WHAT REMAINS, measured:** `torah_trainer` and `classroom_dashboard` need a SECOND lever — a `≤430px`-scoped `header{flex-wrap:wrap}` on top of the row wrap ("lever C"), which took the dashboard 88→**0** and torah 131→**11**; both were verified regression-free at 800/1280 *because of the ≤430 scope*, which is precisely the guard S218's unscoped attempt lacked. **torah's residual 11px is a third, still-unattributed cause** (its right-edge probe names `div.tt-controls` / `span.radio-group`) — attribute that before proposing anything. The generator's `aside#appSidebar.sidebar` is untouched and unattributed.

_(**S190 Pass L** — SEO & discoverability, **5th run**, taken on the S189 pointer's explicit instruction not to defer it a second time. The pointer's method worked exactly as designed: the **byte check ran FIRST** and cleared the copy dimensions in one command (`git diff -U0 1dcd2d7..HEAD -- ':(top)*.html'` → **0 changed lines** matching `<title>`/`name="description"`/`rel="canonical"`/`og:`/`twitter:`/`ld+json`, across **3,538 insertions in 7 root HTML files**), freeing the whole pass to converge on the dimensions that CAN have moved. **Structurally CLEAN for the 5th consecutive run:** canonicals 11/11 with the bare-root homepage; 404/i18n-test noindex + sitemap-absent (**and 404's canonical is still gone — the S174 finding fixed in S175 has not regressed**); sitemap↔canonical **exact 11/11 both directions**; 11/11 `<lastmod>` present; robots.txt Sitemap-directive intent correct with no blanket disallow; **35 JSON-LD blocks parse**; both og/twitter images (`og-card.png`, `og-fontmaker.png`) resolve on disk; titles+descriptions unique with sane lengths (titles 27–61, descriptions 92–164); **exactly one `<h1>` on all 13 pages**; **0 `<img>` without `alt`**; and the **crawl-graph 11/11 reachable from the homepage** (10 direct links, 0 pageerror across the crawl, and the crawl reached nothing outside the indexable set).
**The one dimension a byte check cannot clear produced all 3 findings — "do the claims still match what the tool does".** The copy is byte-identical; the UI beneath it moved 3,538 lines. All three were gate-2 approved and **all three shipped this session** (3237da8): FM's HowTo naming 3 of the wizard's 4 sources (Drawing mode absent) under a step still called "Upload your letters"; the dashboard's Schedule Sync answer describing "a preset and an end time to each period" — precisely the single-day format the tool now calls "your existing schedule" and offers to migrate into the weekly grid; and the dictionary's filter list omitting Browse by Theme.
**The scope discovery that mattered:** each JSON-LD claim has a **VISIBLE FAQ twin** on the same page, and every twin carried the *identical* stale wording in **both languages** — so the structured data was never diverging from the page; the page had fallen behind the tool. Fixing only the JSON-LD would have manufactured a fresh JSON-LD↔visible mismatch, worse than either, so both halves moved together (3 JSON-LD claims + 3 localized EN/HE strings, worded to agree).
**⚑ This REVISES a convention S142's L run ratified.** That run saw the same shape and wrote "newer features like dictionary themes / torah copy simply aren't enumerated, nothing claimed is stale" — i.e. non-enumeration is not staleness. S190 narrows it: a **closed enumeration that asserts the complete set** ("one per image, a full sheet, or an existing font file") IS stale once the set grows, and a description of *how a feature works* that no longer describes it (the dashboard) was never non-enumeration at all. Merely incomplete prose still isn't a hit. The dictionary item is the one S142 explicitly declined; it shipped this time because the maintainer chose all three at gate 2.
**Two would-be findings were KILLED by grounding before either was logged.** (1) og:title/og:description differ from twitter:title/twitter:description on **9 of 11 pages** — **not drift**: every tool page deliberately ships a shorter, brand-suffixed X variant (`Hebrew Flash Cards | IvritSuite`), with 9/11 twitter descriptions shorter than their og twin by design. My equality assertion was stricter than the property the site guarantees and failed 9 correct pages; the real contract (og set complete, `og:url === canonical`, `twitter:image === og:image`, `twitter:card` large) passes 11/11. (2) `404.html` reported a missing canonical as a FAIL — **already fixed in S175** (1c567d4); my diff window simply began after that commit. Both are now regression guards instead of findings.)_

_(**S189 Pass F** — cross-tool consistency, 16th run. Affordance: **busy / loading feedback during a slow operation** — never swept in 15 prior F runs (destructive resets S176, transient toasts S111/S155, share links S133, fullscreen + dark toggles S123, shared-block byte-identity S144/S154, font pickers S80/S100, tour pulses S90, empty states S71). Contract fixed BEFORE measuring, five dimensions: **PRESENT** (visible in-flight text) · **ANNOUNCED** (in a live region) · **CLEARS-OK** · **CLEARS-FAIL** (replaced by an error, never left as "Loading…") · **GUARDED** (no stacked re-fire). Method: each tool's own primary slow resource stalled 2.6–3.0 s and, separately, 502'd; sampled mid-flight and after settle; local HTTP, `serviceWorkers:'block'`.
**Five of seven tools PASS on every dimension:** torah_trainer (`#ttStatus` holds "Loading <label>…" for the whole 2.6 s window, `role=status aria-live=polite`, error line on 502, `_readingLoading`/`_fetchSeq` re-entrancy guard), trope_tutor (`#tuStatus`), font_maker (the most elaborate in the suite — engine badge + phased export-button label + sticky `#statusMsg` + a localized `askModal` on failure), dictionary (visible download progress in KB and %), dashboard (a per-widget line that correctly becomes "Weather unavailable"). **Two laggards; all four findings fixed this session** — see Done.
**Two candidate findings were KILLED by grounding before either was logged.** (1) font_maker's `#statusMsg` appeared to be stuck at "Loading font engine (one-time ~8 MB)…" permanently after a failure — **REFUTED at the reachable path**: all three foreground callers (`exportFont`, `generatePreviewPDF`, `offerSaveToMyFonts`) do `status('')` and then raise a localized error modal, and the background prefetch writes no status at all; my probe had called `ensurePyodide()` with no caller. (2) torah_trainer appeared to show **no** busy text — **a harness fault**: a `waitForFunction` on `#ttParshaSelect option` (that select has no direct `option` children) burned the entire in-flight window before the first sample.
**Informational, deliberately NOT logged as a convergence:** `aria-busy` appears in **0 of 9** files suite-wide. F's own rule treats a uniform absence as enhancement-to-all rather than a laggard, and every carrier already announces through `role="status"`, so `aria-busy` would add nothing.)_

Check your internet connection, or ask your school's IT to allow ivritsuite.com — then reload the page.` HE: `לא ניתן לטעון את אוצר המילים. בדקו את חיבור האינטרנט, או בקשו מצוות המחשוב של בית הספר לאפשר גישה ל-ivritsuite.com — ואז רעננו את הדף.` **Deferred from S190 for budget only (5/5 spent); it is the top open candidate for S191.** | found: 2026-08-08, S189 Pass F; approved 2026-08-08, S190 gate 2
_(**S191 Pass C** — accessibility, one tool: **`torah_trainer.html`**, the S190 pointer's strongly-favored target and its 4th dedicated C (S18 → S72 → S146 → S191). The point of the pick was the three interactive surfaces added since S146 that had never had a dedicated audit — the S190 sticky bulk dock, the S188 type-a-range verse picker, the copy bar — and **the pick paid: 2 of the 3 findings that shipped were defects in the bulk dock itself, one of them a P2.** Structurally the page remains very strong: 124 visible controls with 0 naming defects in either language, 64-stop tab order in a sensible region sequence, a clean drawer focus-trap/Escape/restore contract, 20/20 visible focus rings in both themes, 0 pageerrors everywhere.)_
**The method lesson of the run: the two loudest automated signals were both wrong, and both would have been logged by a less suspicious pass.** (1) "35 of 64 tab stops have no focus indicator" — Chromium computes its own UA focus ring as `outline: <color> auto 0px`, so **`getComputedStyle().outlineWidth` cannot detect a focus ring at all**; a screenshot pixel-diff refuted it 20/20 in both themes. Any future C run that reads styles for this is measuring nothing. (2) "6 tab stops are invisible (0×0)" — those are the toggle-switch `<input>`s, deliberately hidden behind their `.slider`, which the existing `input:focus-visible + .slider` rule rings correctly. **A third near-miss on the verification side:** the fix-verification harness reported the gold **Copy All** button at 1:1 because an opaque `rgb()` parses to 3 components, so an `alpha > 0` guard is `false` for it and every solid-background element gets measured against its ancestor's plate instead of its own fill. The guard must be `alpha === undefined || alpha > 0`.
**And the injection lesson, which cost the first three probe runs:** `currentVerses` is a top-level `let` in a non-module inline script, so it lives in the global **lexical** environment and is **not** a `window` property. `window.currentVerses = …` silently creates a shadowed twin — the harness reported the reading as rendered while `.tt-word` count was 0. A **bare assignment** is the only way in, and `injectReading` now throws on a 0-word receipt rather than proceeding.

- [x] ✅ DONE S194, 1010c0b | P3 | classroom_dashboard.html | **Brand gold measures 2.6–2.75:1 on light surfaces in ten places — and the fix already exists in-suite.** Pass A's contrast measurement (alpha-composited over the nearest opaque ancestor, both themes) flagged 13 dashboard nodes under 3:1; **10 are this one shape**: the 5 footer cross-links ("printable Hebrew worksheets →" …) at `#c9922a` on cream = **2.6:1**, and 5 "active" segmented controls (`עברית`, `MM/DD/YYYY`, `12-hr`, `°F`, `Groups of N`) at the same gold on white = **2.75:1**. **Explicitly NOT the `theme-flipping` class** — dark measures 8.7–11:1, so this is a plain light-mode gap, not a flip. It is also **not a design decision**: `--gold-text` (`#7f5a13` light, `var(--gold)` dark) was authored for exactly this and is already the small-text gold in trope_tutor and torah_trainer, so the fix is a token swap, not a palette change. Check the same tokens on the generator and flash cards before calling it dashboard-only. | found: 2026-08-11, S193 Pass A **⚑ COUNT CORRECTED: 10 → 14.** The 5 footer links are right, but the segmented controls are **8**, not 5 (day-name language and picker mode add three more), plus the first-run tour link. The extra nodes were invisible to S193 because the footer block lives **inside the settings drawer** — the census only completes with the drawer, all 13 panels and all 9 `<details>` forced open. **Generator and flash cards checked as instructed: both already clean** (0 nodes under bar in light; the generator's dark-mode hits are the S193-refuted disabled buttons plus marginal `--muted` sub-labels, logged separately below).

The S193 suite sweep found them **not rendered** in the default state, so the contrast was never measured — enable SVG uploading / the English tab first, measure, then apply the same `--gold-text` one-liner. | found: 2026-08-11, S193 Pass A **⚑ THE GATING CLAIM WAS WRONG, and the correction made the finding bigger:** no mode needs enabling — these are the explanatory text *under* each checkbox and render for anyone on the **Advanced tab** (`setCatTab('advanced')`; the whole `.panel.cat-panel` is `display:none` until then, which is why the S193 sweep saw `rendered:false`). _(**S184 Pass D** — performance, `torah_trainer.html`, its first dedicated D since **S66, 118 sessions ago**. The pass ran as a 5-surface workflow that was KILLED mid-run when the session was suspended: 2 surfaces (boot/render, toggle-churn) reported, 3 (copy+bulk-select scaling, karaoke runtime, session hygiene/leaks) never ran, and the adversarial-verify phase never ran at all. The two headline findings below were therefore re-measured by hand in two separate process runs before being logged — which corrected one of them. **The three unprofiled surfaces are the reason D should be run again soon; see the rotation note.**)_

- [x] ✅ DONE S210, 2947785 (**stale marker corrected S211** — S210 shipped this and the queue box was never ticked) | P3 | torah_trainer.html | **Every transliteration-enabled cold load renders the whole parsha TWICE, and the discarded first pass paints a false "Transliteration unavailable" chip.** `init()` fires `HebrewTransliterator.ensureLoaded()` and then `fetchAndRender()` without waiting, so the first `renderText()` runs while the lazily-imported esm.sh ESM is still in flight: `_translitDead` is true, the reading is built with no translit rows AND a `tt-notice-chip` saying transliteration is unavailable; `wireTranslit`'s `translitlibloaded` handler then rebuilds the entire reading. Measured (S184 Pass D, `#ttReading.innerHTML` setter instrumented, 146 verses, everything on, TWO separate process runs): exactly 2 reading writes both runs — write 1 at t=152/166 ms, 403 KB, 0 translit rows, chip present; write 2 at t=286/309 ms, 498 KB, 146 translit rows, chip gone; gap between paints 134/143 ms at cpu 1 (the profiling agent measured the same shape at 179–412 ms, and 1.4–1.6 s at cpu 4). The wasted pass IS a full `renderText`, so it scales linearly with the reading (5.3/13.4/35.2/75.2 ms at 20/60/146/300 verses). Everything-off loads render exactly ONCE, so this is specific to the translit path. Fix = when `settings.showTranslit`, await `Promise.race([ensureLoaded(), timeout(~2s)])` before the first `renderText`, so the common case renders once and a genuinely blocked CDN still falls through to today's dead-path render. | found: 2026-08-06, S184 Pass D
- [x] ✅ DONE S211, ba9c650 (shipped as the one screen-only rule the entry prescribed; measured ~15x on this harness, not the ~2-4x a 90ms single baseline implied — print PDF byte-identical, scroll convergence + 0px scrollIntoView both verified as the entry demanded) | P3 | torah_trainer.html | **A colour-picker drag, a font-size drag, or the dark toggle restyles the entire reading, so the live preview that makes those controls usable runs at ~10–24 fps on a full parsha.** The JS is already right — S60/S66's var-swap design holds (re-verified S184: `toggleDark` produces **0** `#ttReading` mutations and ~0.3 ms of JS) — the cost is browser restyle over 4,269 `.nik` spans + 1,558 trope-classed words. **PARTIAL, and the correction matters:** the profiling agent reported `toggleDark` at a steady 212–232 ms, but two independent re-measurements gave medians of **352 ms and 161 ms** (samples 148–444 ms) — the baseline is far noisier than one run suggests, so do not quote a single figure. What IS stable across every run is the remedy: adding `#ttReading .tt-verse { content-visibility: auto; contain-intrinsic-size: auto 140px; }` inside `@media screen` measured **90 ms in both runs** (samples 82–98), i.e. a ~2–4× improvement, not the ~7× a single baseline implied. Agent-measured drag frames at 146 verses, cpu 1: font-size slider 95–101 ms/frame (~10 fps), trope picker 49–59 ms (~19 fps), nikkud picker 41–43 ms (~24 fps); ~4–6× worse at cpu 4. Scales linearly with verse count. Fix is one screen-only CSS rule — but it changes scroll/print behaviour of off-screen verses, so verify the print pipeline and the karaoke auto-scroll before landing it. | found: 2026-08-06, S184 Pass D
- [x] ✅ DONE S211, cef62eb (301 -> 9 mutation records, .tt-loop 292 -> 0, exactly the 9 the entry predicted; the third sub-fix — clearLoop's `was` guard — deliberately declined, see the Done entry) | P4 | torah_trainer.html | **`renderLoopState()` rewrites the label and aria-label of every verse's Loop button on every render even when no loop is running — 97% of all mutation records a render produces.** Measured (S184 Pass D, mutation-record attribution, 146 verses, cpu 1): a plain `renderText()` emits 301 records, of which 292 are `childList|BUTTON.tt-loop` (146) + `attributes|BUTTON.tt-loop[aria-label]` (146); the reading rebuild itself accounts for ~9. Corroborated by switching to `biblepage`, which emits no Loop buttons: exactly 9 records. Isolated cost with `loopState.active === false`: 0.5/1.1/2.6/6.1 ms at 20/60/146/300 verses (11.6 ms at 146 / cpu 4), re-dirtying DOM the `innerHTML` write three statements earlier had just built with those exact labels. `syncBulkClasses()` is the same shape at 0.4 ms. Fix = early-return both when there is nothing to do (`!loopState.active` and no `.tt-looping` present; `ttBulkSelected.size === 0` and no `.tt-bulk-selected`), and guard `clearLoop()`'s call with its existing `was` flag. | found: 2026-08-06, S184 Pass D
- [x] ✅ DONE 2026-08-16, 26b9e7e (**found already-fixed on S232 grounding — the candidate outlived its fix by ~4 sessions; it is the `rebuild-where-a-class-swap-would-do` pattern's own hit #1, recorded at the S220 registration but never flipped here. The shipped handler is exactly the proposed swap: applyTropeColors, no renderText, invariant commented at the call site**) | P4 | torah_trainer.html | **The `colorCodeTrope` master toggle forces a full reading rebuild that a body-class swap could avoid — the gating class already exists.** Every trope CSS rule is already gated on `body.trope-on` (`:where(body.trope-on…)` and siblings, plus the print block and `.trope-underline-fallback`), and `applyTropeColors()` already flips that class, all 12 CSS vars and the legend. But `tokenizeHebrew` guards emission of `trope-<fam>` on `settings.colorCodeTrope`, so the toggle takes a rebuild instead. Measured (S184 Pass D, 146 verses, medians of 5): JS 40–42 ms, frame 137–140 ms, longtask 95–98, 301 mutation records each way at cpu 1; ~730 ms frame at cpu 4. Fix = emit the classes unconditionally (the family computation already runs off the ORIGINAL text and is cheap) and change the `#optColorCodeTrope` handler from `renderText()` to `applyTropeColors()`. P4 because it is a once-or-twice-per-session control. | found: 2026-08-06, S184 Pass D
- [ ] P4 (informational — comment correction only) | torah_trainer.html | **`renderText`'s `_translitDead` comment claims `.every()` short-circuits "so when the library is present this costs exactly one extra call" — true on the LIVE path, false on the dead one.** When the library is absent (offline, blocked esm.sh, or the pre-load window of the double-render finding above) `every()` returns true, which can only happen after visiting all N verses, each paying `replaceTetragrammaton(stripTeamim(...))`. Measured at ~0.003 ms/verse — ~1 ms at a full parsha, i.e. below the noise floor. No performance change warranted; worth one comment correction if anyone edits that line. | found: 2026-08-06, S184 Pass D

- [ ] P4 (informational — logged, not changed) | torah_trainer.html | **`textCache` grows one full parsha per distinct reading and is never evicted.** Measured (S185 Pass D, session-hygiene surface, two independent process runs): 12 successive parsha loads → `textCache.size` 12, one entry per `ref|version` key, each holding that reading's complete verse array (146 verses × Hebrew + English strings for a full parsha). It is **bounded by the corpus**, not unbounded — 54 parshiyot × the scopes/translation versions a teacher actually opens — and everything else about repeated loading is provably clean (DOM nodes, listeners and live elements all FLAT across 12 loads). So this is a note for whoever next profiles memory on this page, not a defect: the ceiling is small and the cache is what makes re-picking a parsha instant. | found: 2026-08-06, S185 Pass D

- [x] ✅ DONE S186, cd36f76 (gate-2 approved as drafted at the S186 batch; 2 new shared.fonts.* keys; AX-verified per-row names EN+HE on both carriers) | Hebrew_Font_Maker.html + index.html | **The My Fonts ⬇/🗑 buttons name the ACTION but not the ROW.** S183 (03f64a4) fixed the symbol-only names, so a screen reader no longer hears "🗑" — but ten saved fonts now give ten identical "Remove from My Fonts" with nothing identifying which font each row is. Mechanically trivial (the font name is in scope at both build sites); it needs an approved `{name}` key per carrier, e.g. "Remove {name} from My Fonts" / "Download {name}". `aria-describedby` pointing at the row's name span is a zero-copy alternative, but descriptions are announced late and some readers suppress them. | found: 2026-08-02, S183

_(**Refreshed S166.** The maintainer asked to clear the backlog, and **every open code candidate the
S165 list carried was closed this session** — the 7 Font Maker sliders, both Font Maker P4s, and the

- [x] ✅ DONE S180, 1584f00 | torah_trainer.html | **"Hide nikkud" also deletes the maqaf, the sof pasuk and the paseq — welding words into nonsense on screen, in the clipboard, on the printed student handout, AND shifting the karaoke word indices** (S180 Pass H; **verified four ways with my own receipts, not an agent's**). `NIKKUD_RE = /[ְ-ׇ]/g` is U+05B0–U+05C7, which contains U+05BE maqaf, U+05C0 paseq, U+05C3 sof pasuk and U+05C6 nun hafukha — punctuation, not points. Its own comment says "nikkud only (vowels + dagesh + meteg)", and the sibling `TEAMIM_RE` (U+0591–U+05AF) correctly keeps all four, so this is a bug, not a design call. **Measured:** `stripNikkud("וַיֹּ֥אמֶר … וַֽיְהִי־אֽוֹר׃")` → `"וי֥אמר … ויהיאור"` — two words fused into one nonsense word and the verse-ending ׃ gone; reproduced by driving the real **Show nikkud** toggle, in `verseCopyText()`'s clipboard string, and — snapshotted **at print time** under `body.tt-handout-print` — on the **student handout** (`welded:true, maqaf:false, sofPasuk:false`). **The consequence the walkthrough missed:** `tokenizeHebrew` splits on `(\s+|־)` because PocketTorah counts each side of a maqaf pair as a separate sung word, and `data-twi` indexes the timings array directly — so hiding nikkud drops the sung-word count **27 → 24** (per-verse 7/14/6 → 7/12/5) and every word after the first maqaf highlights against the wrong audio timing. Fix = narrow the class to `[\u05B0-\u05BD\u05BF\u05C1\u05C2\u05C4\u05C5\u05C7]`, keeping the four punctuation codepoints; the `tokenizeHebrew` comment that documents the swallow then needs re-truing (keep the `tropeRealignFamilies` guard — it just stops firing). | found: 2026-08-02, S180 Pass H

- [x] ✅ DONE S180, b7b7b7a | **P3, corrected from P2 by verification** (found INDEPENDENTLY by 2 of 3 walkthroughs; the verifier reproduced the core with a stronger receipt and **refuted two details**) | torah_trainer.html | **Fullscreen — the mode the page sells as SmartBoard projection — ignores all three font-size sliders, and hides every control that could fix it.** `body.fullscreen .tt-heb, body.fullscreen .tt-cell.heb { font-size: 2rem }` beats `.tt-heb { font-size: var(--tt-heb-size) }` on specificity, so pressing the project button makes 2.6rem text **23% SMALLER** (41.6px → 32px) — and it ignores the slider in BOTH directions (0.9rem → 14.4px windowed → 32px fullscreen). **The control is dead with a lying readout:** driven live inside real native fullscreen, `#optHebSizeVal` reported 3.50rem then 0.90rem and `--tt-heb-size` updated both times while the render stayed pinned at 32px. No escape hatch: `body.fullscreen header{display:none}` leaves `#gearBtn` a 0×0 box with no keyboard path, so the teacher must leave fullscreen — where the change then won't apply. **Verifier CORRECTION 1 — "Page view honours the slider" is FALSE**, a measurement artifact (the reporter measured the wrapper `<p class="tt-bible-heb">`, not the `.tt-heb` spans that carry the words): all three layouts are overridden, and page view looks WORST because the 133px drop-cap and the slider-scaled verse superscripts stay put while the body text drops to 32px — so "add `.tt-bible-heb` to the rule" would make it worse. **CORRECTION 2 — scope understated:** translit is overridden in all three layouts too (32→18.4px), and the real layout inconsistency is on the ENGLISH row (`.tt-translation` is in the rule; plain `.tt-cell` and `.tt-bible-translation` are not). **Fix idiom already exists ~150 lines away:** `body.tt-handout-size-large{--tt-heb-size:2.2rem}` — set the body-level VAR, never `font-size` (its own comment explains the var beats `applyFontSizes`' documentElement inline var for every reading element). **P3 not P2:** the setting persists and is restored exactly on exit (measured), and nothing wrong is printed, copied or exported — but it is top-of-band, since the imposed 32px ceiling is too small to read across a room, which is the mode's entire job. Torah is the OUTLIER: the dashboard hardcodes fullscreen sizes too, but its elements have no per-element slider AND it keeps its zoom bar reachable plus ships an explicit ⚙️ Settings button in fullscreen. | found: 2026-08-02, S180 Pass H

- [x] ✅ DONE S185, 30fe852 | torah_trainer.html | **`checkTTSVoiceAvailability()` writes a hardcoded English string** although `torah.audio.tts_no_voice` exists, is translated, and has no call site. **FIXED S185 — and grounding found the naive fix would have shipped a raw key AND a listener leak.** The function runs at init, before `I18n.ready`, and `applyI18n` never re-ran it (the registered **pre-ready-i18n / never-re-rendered** class, 4th instance), so `I18n.t()` alone would have frozen `torah.audio.tts_no_voice` on screen in both languages. The readout therefore moved into a **named** `syncTTSVoiceStatus()` that `applyI18n` calls — named rather than the previous fresh-closure-per-call `check()`, because `speechSynthesis` survives every re-render, so re-running the old shape from `applyI18n` would have stacked a new `voiceschanged` listener on every language switch. Verified EN+HE × light/dark × 1280/800: real Hebrew on screen, and 5× `applyI18n` adds **0** listeners.

_(**S181 Pass G** added the block below — `torah_trainer.html`'s **first-ever dedicated G** (the pointer's named target; G was stalest at S169). Run as a 4-surface parallel workflow (student handout / side-by-side parallel print / interlinear+Page-view+chrome / ink fidelity+clipboard) over **real PDFs through Chromium's print pipeline at Letter AND A4**, page counts read from the PDF bytes. **~24 findings, 7 at P2.** Every finding is print-artifact — the rubric's tie-breaker #2. **The whole set is one tool, so the variety governor caps this session at 2 of them.**)_

- [x] ✅ DONE S181, ce1eab2 | torah_trainer.html | **A4 paper silently discards every Hebrew font-size choice — the handout's three presets AND the teacher's font slider** (S181 Pass G; found INDEPENDENTLY by 3 of 4 surfaces plus my own measurement, then adversarially CONFIRMED with glyph sizes read out of the PDF content streams via PyMuPDF — not print-media emulation). `@media (max-width: 720px)` (~line 1061) is unqualified, so it applies to **print**, and its `.tt-cell.heb, .tt-heb { font-size:1.35rem }` is a literal at the same specificity as the `font-size:var(--tt-heb-size,1.5rem)` base rules (~411/616) but later in source order — so it beats both `applyFontSizes()`'s documentElement var and the handout's `body.tt-handout-size-large/-xl` body override. A4 at Chrome's default margins is 716.9–718.1px, under the 720px breakpoint; Letter at default is 740px and escapes. **Verifier widened it twice:** it fires **unconditionally** (even at the untouched 1.5rem default, A4 prints 1.35rem), and it is **width-driven, not paper-driven** — `A4@none` (794px) prints correctly while `Letter@0.5in` (720px) prints wrong, so US teachers who pick ½-inch margins are hit too. Receipts: handout large/xl = 35.2/44.8px on Letter vs **21.6px on A4**; PDF page counts Letter 2/3/5 vs A4 2/2/2. Severity corrected P1→P2 by the verifier. Page view is immune (`.tt-bible-heb` is not in the mobile rule). **Fix chosen against BOTH agents' recommendation, on measurement:** they proposed `@media screen and (max-width:720px)`; measured on paper that also hands A4 the 24px desktop `.tt-page` padding, shrinking its text column to 670px — 24px narrower than today and 22px narrower than Letter's 692px. Today's narrower mobile padding accidentally compensates for A4's narrower paper (694 vs 692). Restating the var chain inside the print block keeps that and is exactly what the file's S30 end-of-stylesheet source-order convention exists to enable.
- [x] ✅ DONE S181, a4c581d | torah_trainer.html | **The print block's dark-token reset is incomplete, so printing from dark mode fails contrast in two places** (S181 Pass G, ink-fidelity surface). The `html.dark-early body, body.dark { … }` re-statement (~1121-1124) restates 9 tokens but omits **`--gold-text`** and **`--navy-deep`**. (a) `.tt-bnum` (Page-view verse numbers) reads `var(--gold-text)`, which the dark block sets to `var(--gold)` — printing at rgb(201,146,42) = **2.75:1** instead of light #7f5a13's 6.22:1. It is a **text** colour, so it prints on the DEFAULT path regardless of the background-graphics setting. (b) `body.dark .tt-trope-legend{background:var(--navy-deep)}` (0,2,0) out-specifies the print block's `.tt-trope-legend{background:#fff}` (0,1,0) regardless of source order, so with backgrounds ON the legend prints rgb(13,18,32) with **1.30:1** labels.
- [x] ✅ DONE S182, af90983 (**gate-2 ASKED AND ANSWERED at the S182 batch — maintainer chose collapse-AND-explain over silent collapse**) | torah_trainer.html | **With the transliteration library unreachable, the printed sheet still reserves a labelled EMPTY column** (S181 Pass G, found by 2 surfaces + me). `applyTranslit()` returns `''` when the ESM has not loaded and torah_trainer has **no fallback** — unlike hebrew_dictionary, whose identical loader is passed a real corpus `defaultTranslit`. Measured: all 44 `.tt-cell.translit` cells empty while the column keeps **31.4%** of the grid width (57.5mm of 183.1mm) and its header; PDF text runs per band = English 455 / Hebrew 394 / translit **20** (verse labels only); costs an extra sheet in 2 of 2 A4 comparisons. Interlinear is worse — a literal "Translit" label over an empty row on **every** verse. Fix is small (`if (withTranslit && perVerse.every(({v}) => !applyTranslit(v.hebrew))) withTranslit = false;` before `colTranslit`/`colHeb`/`parallel` are computed — they are all computed after `perVerse`, and `.every()` short-circuits when the library IS available) but it makes a column the teacher switched ON disappear — **a default-behaviour change, hence gate 2**. Ask whether silent collapse is acceptable or it should carry a note. Real-world precondition: offline, CDN outage, or a school network blocking esm.sh/jsDelivr.
- [x] ✅ DONE S182, 6896ba5 (**and the characterisation corrected — the wasted sheet carries the reading's TAIL, not nothing; 4/70 PDFs recovered a sheet, 0 regressions**) | torah_trainer.html | **A fully blank trailing sheet — about 1 print in 14** (S181 Pass G). Swept 20/24/28/32/36/40/44 verses × {interlinear, biblepage} × {trope off, on} × {Letter, A4} = **56 real PDFs**, counting text runs per page from the content streams: **4 emitted a final page with ZERO text runs** (interlinear+trope 44v Letter; biblepage 28v Letter; biblepage 32v A4; biblepage+trope 40v Letter). Cause is ~44px of trailing padding. Agent-verified fix: `.tt-page{padding-bottom:0} #ttReading{padding-bottom:0} .tt-bible-blk:last-child{margin-bottom:0} .tt-interlinear .tt-verse:last-child{padding-bottom:0}` in the print block removes all four with no regression.
- [x] ✅ DONE S182, 864c475 (**severity/direction corrected: 0–1px CLEARANCE, not a 2.5px intrusion — two independent methods; and the scope widened to side-by-side, which carries the same 1.7 leading**) | torah_trainer.html | **The printed trope clause underline cuts 2.5px into the te'amim of the line below** (S181 Pass G, interlinear only). A4 marginally worse (−3.0px vs −2.5px). Fix: `body.trope-on .tt-heb { line-height:1.95; }` inside the print block (the leading Page view already has), or drop the print offset.
- [x] ✅ DONE S278, `c704f47` (shipped on this entry's OWN prescribed DOM-grouping shape — but as a print-window reparent, not a renderText change: `_handoutEnter` appends `#ttGenizaMark` into the reading's last verse row after its render, `_handoutExit` re-homes it before its render would wipe it; `.tt-verse`/`.tt-grid-row`'s existing print `break-inside:avoid` does the grouping. **S278 receipts (geniza_probe/geniza_verify):** a synthetic 12-verse handout fired the orphan — ON [705,719,745,**9**] vs OFF [705,719,708], a 9-op 4th sheet existing ONLY for the marker; AFTER: [705,719,578,**176**] — last verse + marker paginate together; n=13–17 byte-identical to before (no-op when the group fits); interlinear + side-by-side × light/dark × 1280/800, double enter/exit round-trips restore the marker home, 0 pageerrors, no PDF page under 40 ink ops.) — was **P3 / size M (re-logged S182; was P2)** | torah_trainer.html | **The לגניזה marker can still be stranded alone on its own sheet, and `break-before:avoid-page` does not stop it.** S182 shipped the drafted fix (`break-before:avoid-page; page-break-before:avoid` on the existing `body.tt-handout-geniza #ttGenizaMark` rule), measured it, and **reverted it**: with the rule shipped vs forcibly disabled the orphan count was **1/13 in BOTH arms**. The marker's predecessor in the box tree is `#ttReading` — a single block already fragmented across pages — not the last verse, so `avoid` has nothing it can legitimately pull back. **Natural repro established (new — S181 did not have one in this form):** interlinear, handout `large`, Letter, **15 verses** → `3p [478,617,6]`, the final sheet carrying only the six letters of לגניזה (geniza OFF at the same settings gives `[478,617,0]`, i.e. the sheet exists either way at that verse count — the marker is what puts INK on it). **S181's own receipt did not reproduce**: Letter n=13 geniza OFF vs ON measured 2 vs 2 pages on the **pre-session baseline** as well as after (0/14 across Letter and A4), so the 2-vs-3 trigger depends on real verse lengths, not just verse count. **A real fix needs a DOM grouping change** — the marker has to share an unbreakable group with the last verse (e.g. rendered inside the final `.tt-verse`, which already carries `break-inside:avoid`), which is a `renderText()` change rather than a CSS declaration. Hence M, not a one-liner. | found: 2026-08-02, S182 measurement of the S181 candidate
- [x] ✅ DONE S182, ede3b22 (spread 158.39px → **0.00px**, pagination unchanged, S53 row-pinning asserted) | torah_trainer.html | **Row separators print as three disjoint stubs, up to 48.2mm apart.** `.tt-grid{align-items:start}` is harmless on screen (grid items are the 3 column wrappers) but in print the wrappers become `display:contents`, so every CELL becomes a grid item and shrinks to its own height while `.tt-print-parallel .tt-grid-row{border-bottom}` draws per row. - [x] ✅ DONE S207, 3aabcea (the print band: a per-sheet running identifier fixed into an enlarged @page top margin — PDF content-stream scan verified Letter 11/11, A4 10/10, HE-dark 11/11 pages) | torah_trainer.html | **The reference header and the trope legend print on sheet 1 only**, so sheets 2..N of a 5–8 sheet parsha are unidentified and, with trope on, un-decodable. - [x] ✅ DONE S204, 8c9d580 (the longhand quartet exactly as prescribed, each longhand keeping the post-S60 model's !important; verified 2px exact at a 40px fixture on screen AND under print emulation, trope line still 4px — hierarchy restored) | P3 | torah_trainer.html | **The nikkud print underline's intended 2px never applies.** `text-decoration: underline solid var(--nik-c) !important` is a *shorthand*, which resets `text-decoration-thickness` to `auto`; the following non-`!important` `text-decoration-thickness:2px` then loses. Proved in isolation (shorthand+!important → auto; without !important → 2px; all-longhand → 2px). Consequence: the nikkud line grows with type size until it equals the 4px trope clause line, collapsing the two-tier hierarchy at handout 'xl'. Fix: use the longhand quartet, matching the trope block's own idiom.

 `rerenderAfterTranslitChange()` tries `renderCustomWordlist` then `renderRealWords` behind `typeof … === 'function'` guards; **both functions were folded into `renderWorksheet` long ago and no longer exist**, so both guards are permanently false. - [x] ✅ DONE (maintainer design-call, c1591a8, 2026-07-22) | Hebrew_Font_Maker.html | **Interactive editing stages given an accessible name** (S125 Pass C). **RESOLVED — a guarded `aria-label` naming the current glyph (`labelStage`) on `#stage`/`#meStage`/`#adStage`; NO role change (the `#nodeBar` keyboard path stays authoritative, so `role="application"`/`"img"` deliberately avoided); refreshed from `applyI18n` (renderStage isn't re-run on a live switch) with an English fallback on a pre-locale key-return. Nuanced: they're editing surfaces, not images, and the *keyboard* path is the separate `#nodeBar` toolbar (roving tabindex, already accessible), so `role="application"` risks trapping SR users and `role="img"` mislabels an interactive surface. A safe slice would be an `aria-label` describing the current glyph (e.g. "Glyph editing canvas — {glyph}") without a role change, but wants a deliberate design call. Otherwise FM audited **a11y-strong** (S125): 0 nameless visible controls after the slider fix, icon buttons carry titles/aria, modal focus-trap via `aOpenModal`, `.me-coord`/`.ad-box` focus-visible indicators present, wrapping-`<label>` coordinate inputs. | found: 2026-07-20, S125 Pass C

- [~] Item (4) ✅ DONE 2026-07-29 (out-of-band, user-directed), b158af4; item (3) remains informational-only | torah_trainer.html | **Pass G print residuals** (S53). **(4) FIXED — side-by-side prints as parallel columns again.** S53 had collapsed it to one column because the two independent block flows broke at different points and produced **wrong verse pairings** (~15 verses out of sync) — truthful, but it cost the parallel text this layout exists for. Restored without the risk: in `@media print` the column wrappers become **`display:contents`** so every cell is a direct grid item, and the builder assigns each cell an **explicit `grid-row` shared across columns**, pinning a verse's Hebrew/translit/translation to the same row so they cannot drift wherever the page breaks. **The trap that made this non-trivial:** the Hebrew column also emits **aliyah dividers**, so it has more children than its siblings and any naive row-index alignment skews — the row map allocates a row per divider (spanning all columns). With no second text there is nothing to align, so that case keeps the single-column collapse rather than reserving an empty half-page. Screen is untouched (`grid-row`/`grid-column` are inert on non-grid-items; measured screen `grid-template-columns` identical before/after). **Method worth reusing: a standalone feasibility probe ran FIRST** — a throwaway page proving Chrome fragments an explicit-placement grid correctly (60 pairs, 4 pages, none split), which de-risked the whole change before a line of production code was written. Then a **negative control** (pre-fix 70/70 rows misaligned + 1 print column → post-fix 0/70 + 3 columns) and a 10-run × light/dark matrix covering 3-col, 2-col, no-parallel, interlinear and bible page; 12-page PDF generated and the rendered page inspected. **(3) still open, and still has no fix:** nikkud letter-mode colours print but collapse to similar greys on B/W — there is no secondary channel available for vowels the way the trope layer got its print underline.

Reducing it further would mean splitting a version's worksheet+answer-key into separate ticks — a change to the strict Worksheet-Chunked-Build contract for a variance-only, throttled-only, extreme, user-initiated case. Parked (same category as the cold-load longtasks). | found: 2026-07-21, S128 Pass D

- [ ] P4 (**NEW S283 — a REFUTATION with receipts, so no future pass re-derives the changelog gap or "fixes" the prefix convention**) | Hebrew_Font_Maker.html + locales/ui-strings.csv | **The Hebrew changelog's release ritual has TWO moving parts a release can silently miss, and S283 repaired the two misses it found.** (1) `CHANGELOG_ORDER` had drifted two releases behind: it headed at `'v5_32'` while `CUR_KEY` pinned v5_34, so v5_33 — which HAS a CSV row — rendered for no non-English reader, exactly the failure the comment above the array describes. Repaired at `351c425`. (2) The CUR_KEY row convention: the CURRENT entry's CSV cell may ship un-prefixed (v5_34 did), but the moment the next release demotes it into the history list — which prints cells verbatim — it needs its `"vN.NN — "` prefix or it becomes a version-less orphan; v5_34's was retrofitted at `351c425`, and v5_35/v5_36 were authored WITH the prefix so no future retrofit is needed. The ritual that keeps this sound: on every release, prepend the OUTGOING CUR_KEY token to `CHANGELOG_ORDER`, point CUR_KEY at the new key, and write the new CSV cell already-prefixed. Do NOT strip the prefixes from current-entry cells to match old style. | found: 2026-08-29, S283
