- [ ] P4 (**NEW S288 Pass L** — method, three probe defects from one run) | (loop method, no file) | **Three probe defects, every one of which would have produced a WRONG conclusion had it gone unchecked.** (1) **A control that mutates a page with nothing to mutate reads as "detector broken".** The img-alt control stripped `alt=` from `resources.html` — which has **zero** static `<img>` tags (the suite's five live on contact ×1, index ×2, torah ×2) — so the arm looked blind until the control was re-targeted at `contact.html`, where it fired instantly. Pick a control's target by measuring the population first, never by assuming a page has the thing. (2) **A control harness whose own baseline is dirty reports every control as firing.** The first mechanical control run copied the pages to a temp root but not the root-level `og-card.png`, so 12 pre-existing "og:image missing" failures swamped the output and every control appeared to pass. Fixed by asserting a **0-failure baseline on the copy** and reporting only the `comm -13` delta against it. (3) **`data-filter="age"` is on the leaf button, not a container** — a `[data-filter="age"] button` selector returned zero chips and made a true claim look unverifiable; the four age buttons each carry `data-filter="age" data-value="…"` themselves. This is the S273 D11 rule again in CSS clothing: read one real element before writing the selector that validates the population. | found: 2026-08-29, S288 Pass L

## Feature seeds (micro-features only; see the Micro-feature track in the session prompt)

_(**Out-of-band seed-intake scan, 2026-08-29 — maintainer-directed ("scene/scan for some more micro-features"), run between sessions after the S288 close-out.** NOT a numbered Pass H: no single tool was walked end-to-end, so it does not reset H's rotation row. Method was the ledger's own highest-yield vein — **cross-tool affordance asymmetry** (an affordance proven in tool A and *provably* absent in tool B) plus an **undiscoverable-shortcut census** — chosen because the bench held **only the two M/L dashboard seeds**, which is exactly why gate 1 auto-skipped SIX consecutive sessions (S278–S288) and why the last five gate-1 offers were no-ops. Every seed below was grounded by reading the real code and names the machinery it reuses, its size, and its storage/CSV cost; none is speculative. Deliberate weighting: **two land in `classroom_dashboard`** (the only tool whose bench was non-empty, and whose two seeds are both M/L — so gate 1 still had nothing S-sized to offer anywhere) and **one in `Hebrew_Font_Maker`**, whose bench has been at **ZERO** since the 2026-08-22 classifier strike and whose last dedicated H was **S97** (~191 sessions — the stalest H target of all seven tools by a factor of two). One byproduct fix-candidate (the 🔗 convergence gap) went to Candidates, not here — it is a defect, not a feature.)_

- [x] ✅ SHIPPED S292, `26f5de8` (**gate-1 chosen from four seeds on its FIRST offer** — the pass that walked this tool is what argued for it: every flow S292 walked starts at that search box. 40 lines in one file, **ZERO new storage keys**, 1 CSV key pair; 45/45 checks incl. an iPhone 13 descriptor and a fresh-profile arm. **The seed's premise was HALF STALE and is corrected here: Escape-to-clear already existed** on the input's inline `onkeydown`, so only the `/`-to-focus half and the `<kbd>` hint were built — plus a Shoresh-mode fallback the seed did not anticipate, so the key is not dead in one of the three modes) | hebrew_dictionary.html | **Press `/` to jump to the search box (Esc to clear it), with a visible `<kbd>` hint.** The dictionary is the suite's one look-it-up-fast tool and it has **no global key handling at all**: `document.addEventListener('keydown'` matches exactly ONCE in the whole file, and that one is `_tourKey`. So a teacher mid-lesson must reach for the mouse for every lookup, and `#searchInput` — the page's only search field — is never reachable from the keyboard without tabbing past the whole header. Shape: one guarded global handler (copy the Font Maker's `udShortcutBlocked()` shape — never fire while typing in a field, never while `_modalKeyHandler`'s word modal or the tour overlay is open), plus a small `<kbd>/</kbd>` hint beside the field under `@media (pointer:fine)` so phones never see a key they don't have. ~25 lines, ZERO new storage keys, 2 CSV keys. Dual-audience: the teacher looking a word up between classes and the student browsing.

Zero new storage keys. | flash_cards.html | **A visible `<kbd>` hint row on the drill screen — and the first home the 1–9 listening keys have ever had.** The card-screen global handler implements SIX shortcuts: Space/Enter flip, ←/→ nav (RTL-aware via `_rtlNav()`), Y/N grading, and **1–9 to pick a listening tile**.   **Two maintainer decisions taken at build time:** ‹/› do BOTH (scroll between dividers on a full parsha; step the Reading picker + re-fetch on a single aliyah; aria-disabled with a stated reason elsewhere), and the bar AUTO-HIDES after 4 s idle rather than sitting permanently dimmed. **The seed's own pointer was already satisfied** — S180's `b7b7b7a` had removed the literal `font-size` from the `body.fullscreen` block, so A−/A+ move `--tt-heb-size` and work; the block's comment says never to re-add it. **Three extractions paid for ~a quarter of the diff**: `syncDarkButtons` (one loop, three carriers, replacing the same two lines copied per button across `toggleDark`+`initDark`, and guarding the header read that was unguarded); `FONT_SIZE_UI` + `setFontSize` (one slider map where there were THREE copies — `resetFontSize`, `syncFormToSettings`' `sizeMap`, the `wireSize` call list — and one writer for a size whatever drives it, clamping from the slider's own `min`/`max` so the bar can never leave the drawer's range); and `aliyahNumForLabel` lifted out of `updateJumpNavState`. **⚑ The bug verification caught, and the lesson:** stepping from `aliyahNumForLabel(_currentVerseLabel)` looked obviously right and was wrong — `jumpToAliyah` CENTRES the divider and the IntersectionObserver then reports the verse just ABOVE it (jump to aliyah 2, `_currentVerseLabel` reads `2:3`), so `›` recomputed the same target forever and could never pass the first divider. A single-press assertion would have passed; the **per-press walk of the whole cycle** (`[1,2,3,4,5,6,6,6,6]`) is what exposed it. Fixed with `fsAliyahIndexAtViewport()`, which reads the dividers' own positions — the section question, not the verse question. **Native `requestFullscreen()` WAS granted headless** (contrary to the plan's assumption), so the `fullscreenchange` listener, the Escape-exit focus rescue and the `#fsExitBtn` round-trip are covered for real, not simulated. Other notable arms: a faded bar does not intercept clicks over the reading (`elementFromPoint` returns a `.tt-word` across the strip — the `pointer-events` split is load-bearing, the wrapper spans `inset-inline:0`); zero extra Sefaria calls on a full-parsha step and exactly one per scope step; `data-twi` byte-identical across a step; `window.alert` never called; the disabled reason survives a language switch (the `applyI18n` re-assert); the bar clears `#fsExitBtn`, `.tt-jumpnav`, the karaoke bar and the hint toast at 1920 and 1280 with karaoke both on and off; `A−`/`A+` needed `dir="ltr"` on the glyphs alone (the sign is bidi-neutral and reordered to `−A` in the Hebrew UI) while the buttons still mirror.

- [x] ✅ SHIPPED (user-directed v5.8 brush-suite extension, 2026-08-07) | Hebrew_Font_Maker.html | **Stylus-tilt control for the Chisel nib.** Fully shipped on top of the v5.8 chisel: an opt-in "Stylus tilt sets the nib angle" checkbox maps the pen's lean azimuth (`atan2(−tiltY, tiltX)` → 0–179°) onto the stroke's `na` at pen-down — frozen per stroke, a deliberate choice over the per-point channel (constant nib angle is the calligraphic contract and keeps strokes erase-split-safe); the slider stays the default for non-tilting pointers. Same build also shipped the visual brush picker (Round/Chisel/Brush/Dotted/Hollow tiles previewing the real stroke engine), speed-sensitive width for mouse/finger, and a tagin/dot/serif Stamp tool. Known tension for the Dotted style + Stamp tool: the user-triggered `doRemoveSpecks` can eat small dots/tagin next to a letter body — undoable; a future pass could skip draw-sourced glyphs. | found: 2026-08-06, Draw-mode build
- [x] ✅ SHIPPED (user-directed v5.8 draw-tools build, 2026-08-07) | Hebrew_Font_Maker.html | **Pixel-level eraser** (erase parts of a stroke, not the whole stroke). Shipped as the Erase tool's default **Partial** mode: the gesture accumulates an erase path with live SVG-mask feedback (array untouched mid-gesture, data-di invariant preserved), then `drawEraseSplit` resamples at ERASE_STEP pitch and splits each stroke into survivor fragments at Rt = eraser R + w/2, one undo entry, DRAW_STROKE_MAX budget so reloads never drop ink. Whole-stroke erase kept as the second sub-mode with its own `drawEraser` size pref. | found: 2026-08-06, Draw-mode build

- [x] ✅ SHIPPED S242, e5c94c1 (**gate-1 chosen over "skip" on maintainer direction "on fontmaker"; 2 iterations, one tool, 84 lines incl. changelog + 5 CSV keys — well inside the ~150-line fence; ZERO new storage keys**, it mutates `l.draw.strokes[].pts` which already rides undo/autosave/`.hfm`, so no AllTools wiring; `FONT_MAKER_VERSION` 5.25 → 5.26 + changelog) | M | Hebrew_Font_Maker.html | ~~**Auto-beautification / snapping of drawn shapes**~~ **SHIPPED S242 (e5c94c1, FM v5.26):** the seed's OWN guard rail ("careful opt-in UX so a child's handwriting isn't sterilized") is what chose the shape — **not** live snapping but a **"Tidy strokes" button** in the Draw panel's whole-letter row, one press → ONE `udDo` entry → Ctrl+Z returns the handwriting byte-for-byte. Two conservative passes: a stroke whose every point lies within 4.5% of its own length of the chord becomes that line; an endpoint within 24 font units of a metric line or user guide moves onto it. **Three groundings made it small:** draw points are ALREADY in font units (`drawFrame` compares them to `em.descender`; `drawRenderCanvas` transforms at scale 1 + y-flip), so `collectSnapTargets()` needs no conversion; the tolerance is a CONSTANT, not `snapTolFont()` (~6 SCREEN px — the same button would do different things at different zooms, and an undoable batch action must be reproducible); and `l.drawSig` is left stale on purpose, since that is already the "strokes no longer match the traced outline" signal that re-arms Save. Plans on COPIES first, so a no-op press pushes no undo entry and claims no work. | found: 2026-08-06, Draw-mode build

Setup-screen "Print card sheet" button lays the generated deck out **3×3 per page with dashed cut lines**; the `sheetDuplex` toggle appends an answer page after each card page. **The duplex mapping is the load-bearing part:** flipping on the long edge reverses left-right, so each ROW is reversed on the answer page, and a short final row is **padded to full width BEFORE reversing** — without the pad, a 2-card row reverses into the wrong columns and every answer on it lands behind the wrong card. One builder covers all modes because every card is `{heb, translit, …}`: Hebrew blends, numbers, colours (cell painted from `colorCss` + `print-color-adjust`; only a literal hex/rgb may reach the style attribute) and emoji all verified. **89 lines** — inside the ~150 ceiling even with duplex, so no split was needed. `sheetDuplex` wired into `getSettings()`/`applySettings()` with `??`, riding the already-registered `hebrewFlashCards_settings` blob (no new AllTools keys).

_(Struck seeds — 2026-08-22, **out-of-band** (between sessions, after the S243 close-out): the maintainer
**struck the Font Maker CNN letter classifier seed** (L, from the 2026-08-06 Draw-mode intake — a
"you're on ג but this looks like נ" check after a draw commit) by explicit direction: not necessary.
Do NOT re-add it; future Pass H runs and gate-1 offers should not re-log this idea. **Grounding captured
at the strike so nobody re-derives it as fresh:** infrastructure was never the obstacle — the suite is
static Pages, `_drawCommitNow` already produces the black-on-white PNG a classifier eats, and the FM CSP
already allows `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` + `cdn.jsdelivr.net`, so it would have been backend-less and free. The
obstacle is **domain mismatch**, and this repo has already paid for that lesson once: fully client-side
Kokoro Hebrew TTS shipped 4032210 and was removed the SAME DAY in 43b64e6 (~2,528 lines deleted)
because "Kokoro is an English-built model … confident, wrong pronunciation in a teaching tool is worse
than none, and the mismatch isn't reliably fixable". A classifier trained on Hebrew *handwriting*, asked
to judge deliberate *font-design* strokes, is that same failure aimed at a different feature. **This
leaves the Font Maker seed bench at ZERO.**)_

- [x] 2026-08-29 | `a71a329` | 404.html | (S291 iter 4 — **GATE 3 ASKED AND APPROVED**, "fix light mode to match the intent") **The 404 hero star reads as the emphasis in light mode, as it already does in dark.** The hero is `4<star>4` with a Star of David as the middle zero. **Dark mode states the intent unambiguously: the star is the BRIGHTEST element at 12.23:1 against numerals at 7.96:1.** Light mode reused those same two tokens — and on a light ground "lighter" means weaker rather than brighter, so the relationship INVERTED: numerals `var(--gold)` at 2.6:1 and star `var(--gold-light)` at **1.41:1**, the star ending up the palest thing on the page instead of the boldest. Both also sat under WCAG's 3:1 large-text floor despite rendering at 176px. The fix **mirrors the dark relationship rather than copying its values** — on cream the emphasis is the DARKER of the pair, so `--hero-star:#7f5a13` (5.88:1) and `--hero-num:#a5741a` (3.88:1); the 1.52× contrast ratio between them matches dark's 1.54×, so both themes say the same thing in their own register. Dark is untouched **by construction** (the dark block maps both new tokens straight back to `var(--gold)`/`var(--gold-light)`) **and by measurement — the before/after dark hero screenshots are BYTE-IDENTICAL**. Honest scope note kept in the commit: the hero div is `aria-hidden="true"` and the page states "page not found" in words below it, so this was never a screen-reader question — it is a visual-intent fix, which is exactly why it went to gate 3 rather than being shipped as a contrast defect. | **Verified** 12 cells (EN/HE × light/dark × 1280/800/390px): 0 pageerrors, 0 horizontal overflow, hero rendered in all 12; measured 3.88:1 / 5.88:1 light and an unchanged 7.96:1 / 12.23:1 dark.

PayPal and Venmo hold **121px** of natural content each, so the floor lifts them to 250 and they match; the Zelle panel holds a QR image and is **279px** naturally, clearing the floor entirely, so the tile went **533 → 562px** on the Zelle tab. **The one panel the floor existed to cover was the one it missed.** Raised to 280px, which clears all three. The panels are already `justify-content:center`, so the extra 30px on PayPal/Venmo distributes evenly rather than pooling at the bottom. **Shrinking the Zelle panel instead was rejected on purpose:** it would mean rendering a QR code people scan with a phone at ~200px. **The floor is a measured constant, not a guess** — natural panel heights are an invariant `[121,121,279]` across all 12 cells (EN/HE × light/dark × 1280/800/420px), the content being a fixed handle, label and button whose translated strings do not change the wrap at any width tested. | **Verified** tile-height jump now **0px in all 12 cells** (was 29px in all 12); surrounding regression matrix (3 prose pages × light/dark × EN/HE × 1280/800px) stays 0 pageerrors / 0 overflow.
